47310 do concurrent (j=js:je, k=1:nz, i=is:ie)
47320 mass = GV%H_to_RZ*h(i,j,k)
47330 sfc_state%ocean_heat(i,j) = sfc_state%ocean_heat(i,j) + mass * CS%tv%T(i,j,k)
4734 enddo
4735 endif
4736361 if (allocated(sfc_state%ocean_salt)) then
47370 do concurrent (j=js:je, i=is:ie) ; sfc_state%ocean_salt(i,j) = 0.0 ; enddo
47380 do concurrent (j=js:je, k=1:nz, i=is:ie)
47390 mass = GV%H_to_RZ*h(i,j,k)
47400 sfc_state%ocean_salt(i,j) = sfc_state%ocean_salt(i,j) + mass * (1.0e-3*CS%tv%S(i,j,k))
4741 enddo
4742 endif
4743 endif
4744
4745361 if (associated(CS%tracer_flow_CSp)) then
4746361 call call_tracer_surface_state(sfc_state, h, G, GV, US, CS%tracer_flow_CSp)
4747 endif
4748
4749361 if (CS%check_bad_sfc_vals) then
47500 numberOfErrors=0 ! count number of errors
4751 !$omp target update from(sfc_state%sea_lev, sfc_state%u, sfc_state%v)
4752 !$omp target update if(use_temperature) from(sfc_state%SSS, sfc_state%SST)
47530 do j=js,je ; do i=is,ie
47540 if (G%mask2dT(i,j)>0.) then
4755 localError = sfc_state%sea_lev(i,j) < -G%bathyT(i,j) - G%Z_ref &
4756 .or. sfc_state%sea_lev(i,j) >= CS%bad_val_ssh_max + (G%meanSL(i,j) - G%Z_ref) &
4757 .or. sfc_state%sea_lev(i,j) <= -CS%bad_val_ssh_max + (G%meanSL(i,j) - G%Z_ref) &
47580 .or. sfc_state%sea_lev(i,j) + G%bathyT(i,j) + G%Z_ref < CS%bad_val_col_thick
47590 if (use_temperature) localError = localError &
4760 .or. sfc_state%SSS(i,j)<0. &
4761 .or. sfc_state%SSS(i,j)>=CS%bad_val_sss_max &
4762 .or. sfc_state%SST(i,j)< CS%bad_val_sst_min &
47630 .or. sfc_state%SST(i,j)>=CS%bad_val_sst_max
47640 if (localError) then
47650 numberOfErrors=numberOfErrors+1
47660 if (numberOfErrors<9) then ! Only report details for the first few errors
47670 ig = i + G%HI%idg_offset ! Global i-index
47680 jg = j + G%HI%jdg_offset ! Global j-index
47690 if (use_temperature) then
4770 write(msg(1:240),'(2(a,I0,1x),4(a,f8.3,1x),8(a,es11.4,1x))') &
47710 'Extreme surface sfc_state detected: i=',ig,'j=',jg, &
47720 'lon=',G%geoLonT(i,j), 'lat=',G%geoLatT(i,j), &
47730 'x=',G%gridLonT(ig), 'y=',G%gridLatT(jg), &
47740 'D=',US%Z_to_m*(G%bathyT(i,j)+G%Z_ref), 'SSH=',US%Z_to_m*sfc_state%sea_lev(i,j), &
47750 'SST=',US%C_to_degC*sfc_state%SST(i,j), 'SSS=',US%S_to_ppt*sfc_state%SSS(i,j), &
47760 'U-=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%u(I-1,j), 'U+=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%u(I,j), &
47770 'V-=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%v(i,J-1), 'V+=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%v(i,J)
4778 else
4779 write(msg(1:240),'(2(a,I0,1x),4(a,f8.3,1x),6(a,es11.4))') &
47800 'Extreme surface sfc_state detected: i=',ig,'j=',jg, &
47810 'lon=',G%geoLonT(i,j), 'lat=',G%geoLatT(i,j), &
47820 'x=',G%gridLonT(ig), 'y=',G%gridLatT(jg), &
47830 'D=',US%Z_to_m*(G%bathyT(i,j)+G%Z_ref), 'SSH=',US%Z_to_m*sfc_state%sea_lev(i,j), &
47840 'U-=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%u(I-1,j), 'U+=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%u(I,j), &
47850 'V-=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%v(i,J-1), 'V+=',US%L_T_to_m_s*sfc_state%v(i,J)
4786 endif
47870 call MOM_error(WARNING, trim(msg), all_print=.true.)
47880 elseif (numberOfErrors==9) then ! Indicate once that there are more errors
47890 call MOM_error(WARNING, 'There were more unreported extreme events!', all_print=.true.)
4790 endif ! numberOfErrors
4791 endif ! localError
4792 endif ! mask2dT
4793 enddo ; enddo
47940 call sum_across_PEs(numberOfErrors)
47950 if (numberOfErrors>0) then
47960 write(msg(1:240),'(a,i0,a)') 'There were a total of ',numberOfErrors, &
47970 ' locations detected with extreme surface values!'
47980 call MOM_error(FATAL, trim(msg))
4799 endif
4800 endif
4801
4802361 if (CS%debug) then
4803 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%sea_lev)) from(sfc_state%sea_lev)
4804 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%Hml)) from(sfc_state%Hml)
4805 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%u)) from(sfc_state%u)
4806 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%v)) from(sfc_state%v)
4807 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%SSS)) from(sfc_state%SSS)
4808 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%SST)) from(sfc_state%SST)
4809 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%frazil)) from(sfc_state%frazil)
4810 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%melt_potential)) from(sfc_state%melt_potential)
4811 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%ocean_mass)) from(sfc_state%ocean_mass)
4812 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%ocean_heat)) from(sfc_state%ocean_heat)
4813 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state%ocean_salt)) from(sfc_state%ocean_salt)
48140 call MOM_surface_chksum("Post extract_sfc", sfc_state, G, US, haloshift=0, symmetric=.true.)
4815 endif
4816
4817 ! Rotate sfc_state back onto the input grid, sfc_state_in
4818361 if (CS%rotate_index) then
4819 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%sea_lev)) map(from: sfc_state%sea_lev)
4820 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%Hml)) map(from: sfc_state%Hml)
4821 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%u)) map(from: sfc_state%u)
4822 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%v)) map(from: sfc_state%v)
4823 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%SSS)) map(from: sfc_state%SSS)
4824 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%SST)) map(from: sfc_state%SST)
4825 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%sfc_density)) map(from: sfc_state%sfc_density)
4826 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%frazil)) map(from: sfc_state%frazil)
4827 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%melt_potential)) map(from: sfc_state%melt_potential)
4828 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%ocean_mass)) map(from: sfc_state%ocean_mass)
4829 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%ocean_heat)) map(from: sfc_state%ocean_heat)
4830 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%ocean_salt)) map(from: sfc_state%ocean_salt)
4831 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%taux_shelf)) map(from: sfc_state%taux_shelf)
4832 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%tauy_shelf)) map(from: sfc_state%tauy_shelf)
4833 !$omp target exit data if(allocated(sfc_state%fco2)) map(from: sfc_state%fco2)
4834 !$omp target exit data map(delete: sfc_state)
48350 call rotate_surface_state(sfc_state, sfc_state_in, G_in, -turns)
48360 call deallocate_surface_state(sfc_state)
4837 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%sea_lev)) to(sfc_state_in%sea_lev)
4838 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%Hml)) to(sfc_state_in%Hml)
4839 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%u)) to(sfc_state_in%u)
4840 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%v)) to(sfc_state_in%v)
4841 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%SSS)) to(sfc_state_in%SSS)
4842 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%SST)) to(sfc_state_in%SST)
4843 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%sfc_density)) to(sfc_state_in%sfc_density)
4844 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%frazil)) to(sfc_state_in%frazil)
4845 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%melt_potential)) to(sfc_state_in%melt_potential)
4846 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%ocean_mass)) to(sfc_state_in%ocean_mass)
4847 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%ocean_heat)) to(sfc_state_in%ocean_heat)
4848 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%ocean_salt)) to(sfc_state_in%ocean_salt)
4849 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%taux_shelf)) to(sfc_state_in%taux_shelf)
4850 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%tauy_shelf)) to(sfc_state_in%tauy_shelf)
4851 !$omp target update if(allocated(sfc_state_in%fco2)) to(sfc_state_in%fco2)
4852 endif
4853 !$omp target exit data if(use_temperature) map(release: CS%tv%T, CS%tv%S)
4854 !$omp target exit data &
4855 !$omp if (use_temperature .or. (allocated(sfc_state%frazil) .and. associated(CS%tv%frazil))) &
4856 !$omp map(release: CS%tv)
4857 !$omp target exit data map(release: CS)
4858
4859361 call callTree_leave("extract_surface_sfc_state()")
4860361end subroutine extract_surface_state

4862!> Rotate initialization fields from input to rotated arrays.
48630subroutine rotate_initial_state(u_in, v_in, h_in, T_in, S_in, &
48640 use_temperature, turns, u, v, h, T, S)
4865 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(in) :: u_in !< Zonal velocity on the initial grid [L T-1 ~> m s-1]
4866 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(in) :: v_in !< Meridional velocity on the initial grid [L T-1 ~> m s-1]
4867 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(in) :: h_in !< Layer thickness on the initial grid [H ~> m or kg m-2]
4868 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(in) :: T_in !< Temperature on the initial grid [C ~> degC]
4869 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(in) :: S_in !< Salinity on the initial grid [S ~> ppt]
4870 logical, intent(in) :: use_temperature !< If true, temperature and salinity are active
4871 integer, intent(in) :: turns !< The number quarter-turns to apply
4872 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(out) :: u !< Zonal velocity on the rotated grid [L T-1 ~> m s-1]
4873 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(out) :: v !< Meridional velocity on the rotated grid [L T-1 ~> m s-1]
4874 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(out) :: h !< Layer thickness on the rotated grid [H ~> m or kg m-2]
4875 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(out) :: T !< Temperature on the rotated grid [C ~> degC]
4876 real, dimension(:,:,:), intent(out) :: S !< Salinity on the rotated grid [S ~> ppt]
4877
48780 call rotate_vector(u_in, v_in, turns, u, v)
48790 call rotate_array(h_in, turns, h)
48800 if (use_temperature) then
48810 call rotate_array(T_in, turns, T)
48820 call rotate_array(S_in, turns, S)
4883 endif
48840end subroutine rotate_initial_state

4886!> Return true if all phases of step_MOM are at the same point in time.
48871081function MOM_state_is_synchronized(CS, adv_dyn) result(in_synch)
4888 type(MOM_control_struct), intent(inout) :: CS !< MOM control structure
4889 logical, optional, intent(in) :: adv_dyn !< If present and true, only check
4890 !! whether the advection is up-to-date with
4891 !! the dynamics.
4892 logical :: in_synch !< True if all phases of the update are synchronized.
4893
4894 logical :: adv_only
4895
48961081 adv_only = .false. ; if (present(adv_dyn)) adv_only = adv_dyn
4897
48981081 if (adv_only) then
48990 in_synch = (CS%t_dyn_rel_adv == 0.0)
4900 else
49011081 in_synch = ((CS%t_dyn_rel_adv == 0.0) .and. (CS%t_dyn_rel_thermo == 0.0))
4902 endif
4903
49041081end function MOM_state_is_synchronized

4906!> This subroutine offers access to values or pointers to other types from within
4907!! the MOM_control_struct, allowing the MOM_control_struct to be opaque.
49081subroutine get_MOM_state_elements(CS, G, GV, US, C_p, C_p_scaled, use_temp)
4909 type(MOM_control_struct), intent(inout), target :: CS !< MOM control structure
4910 type(ocean_grid_type), optional, pointer :: G !< structure containing metrics and grid info
4911 type(verticalGrid_type), optional, pointer :: GV !< structure containing vertical grid info
4912 type(unit_scale_type), optional, pointer :: US !< A dimensional unit scaling type
4913 real, optional, intent(out) :: C_p !< The heat capacity [J kg degC-1]
4914 real, optional, intent(out) :: C_p_scaled !< The heat capacity in scaled
4915 !! units [Q C-1 ~> J kg-1 degC-1]
4916 logical, optional, intent(out) :: use_temp !< True if temperature is a state variable
4917
49181 if (present(G)) G => CS%G_in
49191 if (present(GV)) GV => CS%GV
49201 if (present(US)) US => CS%US
49211 if (present(C_p)) C_p = CS%US%Q_to_J_kg*US%degC_to_C * CS%tv%C_p
49221 if (present(C_p_scaled)) C_p_scaled = CS%tv%C_p
49231 if (present(use_temp)) use_temp = associated(CS%tv%T)
49241end subroutine get_MOM_state_elements

4926!> Find the global integrals of various quantities.
49270subroutine get_ocean_stocks(CS, mass, heat, salt, on_PE_only)
4928 type(MOM_control_struct), intent(inout) :: CS !< MOM control structure
4929 real, optional, intent(out) :: heat !< The globally integrated integrated ocean heat [J].
4930 real, optional, intent(out) :: salt !< The globally integrated integrated ocean salt [kg].
4931 real, optional, intent(out) :: mass !< The globally integrated integrated ocean mass [kg].
4932 logical, optional, intent(in) :: on_PE_only !< If present and true, only sum on the local PE.
4933
49340 if (present(mass)) &
49350 mass = global_mass_integral(CS%h, CS%G, CS%GV, on_PE_only=on_PE_only)
49360 if (present(heat)) &
4937 heat = CS%US%Q_to_J_kg*CS%US%RZL2_to_kg * CS%tv%C_p * &
49380 global_mass_integral(CS%h, CS%G, CS%GV, CS%tv%T, on_PE_only=on_PE_only, tmp_scale=CS%US%C_to_degC)
49390 if (present(salt)) &
49400 salt = 1.0e-3 * global_mass_integral(CS%h, CS%G, CS%GV, CS%tv%S, on_PE_only=on_PE_only, unscale=CS%US%S_to_ppt)
4941
49420end subroutine get_ocean_stocks

4945!> Save restart/pickup files required to initialize the MOM6 internal state.
49461subroutine save_MOM_restart(CS, directory, time, G, time_stamped, filename, &
4947 GV, num_rest_files, write_IC)
4948 type(MOM_control_struct), intent(inout) :: CS
4949 !< MOM control structure
4950 character(len=*), intent(in) :: directory
4951 !< The directory where the restart files are to be written
4952 type(time_type), intent(in) :: time
4953 !< The current model time
4954 type(ocean_grid_type), intent(inout) :: G
4955 !< The ocean's grid structure
4956 logical, optional, intent(in) :: time_stamped
4957 !< If present and true, add time-stamp to the restart file names
4958 character(len=*), optional, intent(in) :: filename
4959 !< A filename that overrides the name in CS%restartfile
4960 type(verticalGrid_type), optional, intent(in) :: GV
4961 !< The ocean's vertical grid structure
4962 integer, optional, intent(out) :: num_rest_files
4963 !< number of restart files written
4964 logical, optional, intent(in) :: write_IC
4965 !< If present and true, initial conditions are being written
4966
4967 logical :: showCallTree
49681 showCallTree = callTree_showQuery()
4969
49701 call cpu_clock_begin(id_clock_ocean) ; call cpu_clock_begin(id_clock_save_restart)
49711 if (showCallTree) call callTree_waypoint("About to call save_restart (step_MOM)")
4972 call save_restart(directory, time, G, CS%restart_CS, &
4973 time_stamped=time_stamped, filename=filename, GV=GV, &
49741 num_rest_files=num_rest_files, write_IC=write_IC)
49751 if (showCallTree) call callTree_waypoint("Done with call to save_restart (step_MOM)")
4976
49771 if (CS%use_particles) call particles_save_restart(CS%particles, CS%h, directory, time, time_stamped)
49781 call cpu_clock_end(id_clock_save_restart) ; call cpu_clock_end(id_clock_ocean)

4982!> End of ocean model, including memory deallocation
49831subroutine MOM_end(CS)
4984 type(MOM_control_struct), intent(inout) :: CS !< MOM control structure
4985
49861 call cpu_clock_begin(id_clock_ocean) ; call cpu_clock_begin(id_clock_MOM_end)
4987
49881 call MOM_sum_output_end(CS%sum_output_CSp)
4989
49901 if (CS%use_ALE_algorithm) call ALE_end(CS%ALE_CSp)
4991
4992 !deallocate porous topography variables
4993 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%pbv%por_face_areaU, CS%pbv%por_face_areaV)
49941 deallocate(CS%pbv%por_face_areaU) ; deallocate(CS%pbv%por_face_areaV)
4995 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%pbv%por_layer_widthU, CS%pbv%por_layer_widthV)
49961 deallocate(CS%pbv%por_layer_widthU) ; deallocate(CS%pbv%por_layer_widthV)
4997
4998 ! NOTE: Allocated in PressureForce_FV_Bouss
49991 if (associated(CS%tv%varT)) deallocate(CS%tv%varT)
5000
50011 call tracer_advect_end(CS%tracer_adv_CSp)
50021 call tracer_hor_diff_end(CS%tracer_diff_CSp)
50031 call tracer_registry_end(CS%tracer_Reg)
50041 call tracer_flow_control_end(CS%tracer_flow_CSp)
5005
50061 if (.not. CS%adiabatic) then
50070 call diabatic_driver_end(CS%diabatic_CSp)
50080 deallocate(CS%diabatic_CSp)
5009 endif
5010
50111 call MOM_diagnostics_end(CS%diagnostics_CSp, CS%ADp, CS%CDp)
5012
50131 if (CS%offline_tracer_mode) call offline_transport_end(CS%offline_CSp)
5014
50151 if (CS%split .and. CS%use_alt_split) then
50160 call end_dyn_split_RK2b(CS%dyn_split_RK2b_CSp)
50171 elseif (CS%split) then
50181 call end_dyn_split_RK2(CS%dyn_split_RK2_CSp)
50190 elseif (CS%use_RK2) then
50200 call end_dyn_unsplit_RK2(CS%dyn_unsplit_RK2_CSp)
5021 else
50220 call end_dyn_unsplit(CS%dyn_unsplit_CSp)
5023 endif
5024
50251 if (CS%use_particles) then
50260 call particles_end(CS%particles, CS%h)
50270 deallocate(CS%particles)
5028 endif
5029
50301 call thickness_diffuse_end(CS%thickness_diffuse_CSp, CS%CDp)
50311 if (CS%interface_filter) call interface_filter_end(CS%interface_filter_CSp, CS%CDp)
50321 call VarMix_end(CS%VarMix)
5033 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%VarMix)
5034
50351 call set_visc_end(CS%visc, CS%set_visc_CSp)
5036 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%visc, CS%set_visc_CSp)
50371 deallocate(CS%set_visc_CSp)
50381 deallocate(CS%visc)
5039
50401 call MEKE_end(CS%MEKE)
5041
50421 if (associated(CS%tv%internal_heat)) deallocate(CS%tv%internal_heat)
50431 if (associated(CS%tv%TempxPmE)) deallocate(CS%tv%TempxPmE)
5044
50451 DEALLOC_(CS%ave_ssh_ibc) ; DEALLOC_(CS%ssh_rint) ; DEALLOC_(CS%eta_av_bc)
5046 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%ave_ssh_ibc)
5047 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%ssh_rint)
5048 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%eta_av_bc)
5049
5050 ! TODO: debug_truncations deallocation
5051
50521 DEALLOC_(CS%uhtr) ; DEALLOC_(CS%vhtr)
5053 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%uhtr, CS%vhtr)
5054
50551 if (allocated(CS%uhtr_resolved)) deallocate(CS%uhtr_resolved)
50561 if (allocated(CS%vhtr_resolved)) deallocate(CS%vhtr_resolved)
5057
50581 if (associated(CS%Hml)) deallocate(CS%Hml)
50591 if (associated(CS%tv%salt_deficit)) deallocate(CS%tv%salt_deficit)
50601 if (associated(CS%tv%frazil)) deallocate(CS%tv%frazil)
50611 if (allocated(CS%tv%SpV_avg)) deallocate(CS%tv%SpV_avg)
5062
50631 if (associated(CS%tv%T)) then
50640 DEALLOC_(CS%T) ; CS%tv%T => NULL() ; DEALLOC_(CS%S) ; CS%tv%S => NULL()
5065 endif
5066
50671 DEALLOC_(CS%u) ; DEALLOC_(CS%v) ; DEALLOC_(CS%h)
50681 DEALLOC_(CS%uh) ; DEALLOC_(CS%vh)
5069 !$omp target exit data map(delete: CS%u, CS%v, CS%h, CS%uh, CS%vh)
5070
50711 if (associated(CS%update_OBC_CSp)) call OBC_register_end(CS%update_OBC_CSp)
50721 if (associated(CS%OBC)) call open_boundary_end(CS%OBC)
5073
50741 call verticalGridEnd(CS%GV)
50751 call MOM_grid_end(CS%G)
5076
50771 if (CS%debug .or. CS%G%symmetric) &
50781 call deallocate_MOM_domain(CS%G%Domain_aux)
5079
50801 if (CS%rotate_index) &
50810 call deallocate_MOM_domain(CS%G%Domain)
5082
5083 ! The MPP domains may be needed by an external coupler, so use `cursory`.
5084 ! TODO: This may create a domain memory leak, and needs investigation.
50851 call deallocate_MOM_domain(CS%G_in%domain, cursory=.true.)
5086
50871 call unit_scaling_end(CS%US)
5088
50891 call cpu_clock_end(id_clock_MOM_end) ; call cpu_clock_end(id_clock_ocean)
5090
50911end subroutine MOM_end

5120!!
5121!! \section section_overview Overview of MOM
5122!!
5123!! This program (MOM) simulates the ocean by numerically solving
5124!! the hydrostatic primitive equations in generalized Lagrangian
5125!! vertical coordinates, typically tracking stretched pressure (p*)
5126!! surfaces or following isopycnals in the ocean's interior, and
5127!! general orthogonal horizontal coordinates. Unlike earlier versions
5128!! of MOM, in MOM6 these equations are horizontally discretized on an
5129!! Arakawa C-grid. (It remains to be seen whether a B-grid dynamic
5130!! core will be revived in MOM6 at a later date; for now applications
5131!! requiring a B-grid discretization should use MOM5.1.) MOM6 offers
5132!! a range of options for the physical parameterizations, from those
5133!! most appropriate to highly idealized models for geophysical fluid
5134!! dynamics studies to a rich suite of processes appropriate for
5135!! realistic ocean simulations. The thermodynamic options typically
5136!! use conservative temperature and preformed salinity as conservative
5137!! state variables and a full nonlinear equation of state, but there
5138!! are also idealized adiabatic configurations of the model that use
5139!! fixed density layers. Version 6.0 of MOM continues in the long
5140!! tradition of a commitment to climate-quality ocean simulations
5141!! embodied in previous versions of MOM, even as it draws extensively
5142!! on the lessons learned in the development of the Generalized Ocean
5143!! Layered Dynamics (GOLD) ocean model, which was also primarily
5144!! developed at NOAA/GFDL. MOM has also benefited tremendously from
5145!! the FMS infrastructure, which it utilizes and shares with other
5146!! component models developed at NOAA/GFDL.
5147!!
5148!! When run is isopycnal-coordinate mode, the uppermost few layers
5149!! are often used to describe a bulk mixed layer, including the
5150!! effects of penetrating shortwave radiation. Either a split-
5151!! explicit time stepping scheme or a non-split scheme may be used
5152!! for the dynamics, while the time stepping may be split (and use
5153!! different numbers of steps to cover the same interval) for the
5154!! forcing, the thermodynamics, and for the dynamics. Most of the
5155!! numerics are second order accurate in space. MOM can run with an
5156!! absurdly thin minimum layer thickness. A variety of non-isopycnal
5157!! vertical coordinate options are under development, but all exploit
5158!! the advantages of a Lagrangian vertical coordinate, as discussed
5159!! in detail by Adcroft and Hallberg (Ocean Modelling, 2006).
5160!!
5161!! Details of the numerics and physical parameterizations are
5162!! provided in the appropriate source files. All of the available
5163!! options are selected at run-time by parsing the input files,
5164!! usually MOM_input and MOM_override, and the options choices are
5165!! then documented for each run in MOM_param_docs.
5166!!
5167!! MOM6 integrates the equations forward in time in three distinct
5168!! phases. In one phase, the dynamic equations for the velocities
5169!! and layer thicknesses are advanced, capturing the propagation of
5170!! external and internal inertia-gravity waves, Rossby waves, and
5171!! other strictly adiabatic processes, including lateral stresses,
5172!! vertical viscosity and momentum forcing, and interface height
5173!! diffusion (commonly called Gent-McWilliams diffusion in depth-
5174!! coordinate models). In the second phase, all tracers are advected
5175!! and diffused along the layers. The third phase applies diabatic
5176!! processes, vertical mixing of water properties, and perhaps
5177!! vertical remapping to cause the layers to track the desired
5178!! vertical coordinate.
5179!!
5180!! The present file (MOM.F90) orchestrates the main time stepping
5181!! loops. One time integration option for the dynamics uses a split
5182!! explicit time stepping scheme to rapidly step the barotropic
5183!! pressure and velocity fields. The barotropic velocities are
5184!! averaged over the baroclinic time step before they are used to
5185!! advect thickness and determine the baroclinic accelerations. As
5186!! described in Hallberg and Adcroft (2009), a barotropic correction
5187!! is applied to the time-mean layer velocities to ensure that the
5188!! sum of the layer transports agrees with the time-mean barotropic
5189!! transport, thereby ensuring that the estimates of the free surface
5190!! from the sum of the layer thicknesses agrees with the final free
5191!! surface height as calculated by the barotropic solver. The
5192!! barotropic and baroclinic velocities are kept consistent by
5193!! recalculating the barotropic velocities from the baroclinic
5194!! transports each time step. This scheme is described in Hallberg,
5195!! 1997, J. Comp. Phys. 135, 54-65 and in Hallberg and Adcroft, 2009,
5196!! Ocean Modelling, 29, 15-26.
5197!!
5198!! The other time integration options use non-split time stepping
5199!! schemes based on the 3-step third order Runge-Kutta scheme
5200!! described in Matsuno, 1966, J. Met. Soc. Japan, 44, 85-88, or on
5201!! a two-step quasi-2nd order Runge-Kutta scheme. These are much
5202!! slower than the split time-stepping scheme, but they are useful
5203!! for providing a more robust solution for debugging cases where the
5204!! more complicated split time-stepping scheme may be giving suspect
5205!! solutions.
5206!!
5207!! There are a range of closure options available. Horizontal
5208!! velocities are subject to a combination of horizontal biharmonic
5209!! and Laplacian friction (based on a stress tensor formalism) and a
5210!! vertical Fickian viscosity (perhaps using the kinematic viscosity
5211!! of water). The horizontal viscosities may be constant, spatially
5212!! varying or may be dynamically calculated using Smagorinsky's
5213!! approach. A diapycnal diffusion of density and thermodynamic
5214!! quantities is also allowed, but not required, as is horizontal
5215!! diffusion of interface heights (akin to the Gent-McWilliams
5216!! closure of geopotential coordinate models). The diapycnal mixing
5217!! may use a fixed diffusivity or it may use the shear Richardson
5218!! number dependent closure, like that described in Jackson et al.
5219!! (JPO, 2008). When there is diapycnal diffusion, it applies to
5220!! momentum as well. As this is in addition to the vertical viscosity,
5221!! the vertical Prandtl always exceeds 1. A refined bulk-mixed layer
5222!! is often used to describe the planetary boundary layer in realistic
5223!! ocean simulations.
5224!!
5225!! MOM has a number of noteworthy debugging capabilities.
5226!! Excessively large velocities are truncated and MOM will stop
5227!! itself after a number of such instances to keep the model from
5228!! crashing altogether. This is useful in diagnosing failures,
5229!! or (by accepting some truncations) it may be useful for getting
5230!! the model past the adjustment from an ill-balanced initial
5231!! condition. In addition, all of the accelerations in the columns
5232!! with excessively large velocities may be directed to a text file.
5233!! Parallelization errors may be diagnosed using the DEBUG option,
5234!! which causes extensive checksums to be written out along with
5235!! comments indicating where in the algorithm the sums originate and
5236!! what variable is being summed. The point where these checksums
5237!! differ between runs is usually a good indication of where in the
5238!! code the problem lies. All of the test cases provided with MOM
5239!! are routinely tested to ensure that they give bitwise identical
5240!! results regardless of the domain decomposition, or whether they
5241!! use static or dynamic memory allocation.
5242!!
5243!! \section section_structure Structure of MOM
5244!!
5245!! About 115 other files of source code and 4 header files comprise
5246!! the MOM code, although there are several hundred more files that
5247!! make up the FMS infrastructure upon which MOM is built. Each of
5248!! the MOM files contains comments documenting what it does, and
5249!! most of the file names are fairly self-evident. In addition, all
5250!! subroutines and data types are referenced via a module use, only
5251!! statement, and the module names are consistent with the file names,
5252!! so it is not too hard to find the source file for a subroutine.
5253!!
5254!! The typical MOM directory tree is as follows:
5255!!
5256!! \verbatim
5257!! ../MOM
5258!! |-- ac
5259!! |-- config_src
5260!! | |-- drivers
5261!! | ! |-- FMS_cap
5262!! | ! |-- ice_solo_driver
5263!! | ! |-- mct_cap
5264!! | ! |-- nuopc_cap
5265!! | ! |-- solo_driver
5266!! | ! `-- unit_drivers
5267!! | |-- external
5268!! | ! |-- drifters
5269!! | ! |-- GFDL_ocean_BGC
5270!! | ! `-- ODA_hooks
5271!! | |-- infra
5272!! | ! |-- FMS1
5273!! | ! `-- FMS2
5274!! | `-- memory
5275!! | ! |-- dynamic_nonsymmetric
5276!! | ! `-- dynamic_symmetric
5277!! |-- docs
5278!! |-- pkg
5279!! | |-- CVMix-src
5280!! | |-- ...
5281!! | `-- MOM6_DA_hooks
5282!! `-- src
5283!! |-- ALE
5284!! |-- core
5285!! |-- diagnostics
5286!! |-- equation_of_state
5287!! |-- framework
5288!! |-- ice_shelf
5289!! |-- initialization
5290!! |-- ocean_data_assim
5291!! |-- parameterizations
5292!! | |-- CVMix
5293!! | |-- lateral
5294!! | `-- vertical
5295!! |-- tracer
5296!! `-- user
5297!! \endverbatim
5298!!
5299!! Rather than describing each file here, selected directory contents
5300!! will be described to give a broad overview of the MOM code
5301!! structure.
5302!!
5303!! The directories under config_src contain files that are used for
5304!! configuring the code, for instance for coupled or ocean-only runs.
5305!! Only one or two of these directories are used in compiling any,
5306!! particular run.
5307!!
5308!! * config_src/drivers/FMS-cap:
5309!! The files here are used to couple MOM as a component in a larger
5310!! run driven by the FMS coupler. This includes code that converts
5311!! various forcing fields into the code structures and flux and unit
5312!! conventions used by MOM, and converts the MOM surface fields
5313!! back to the forms used by other FMS components.
5314!!
5315!! * config_src/drivers/nuopc-cap:
5316!! The files here are used to couple MOM as a component in a larger
5317!! run driven by the NUOPC coupler. This includes code that converts
5318!! various forcing fields into the code structures and flux and unit
5319!! conventions used by MOM, and converts the MOM surface fields
5320!! back to the forms used by other NUOPC components.
5321!!
5322!! * config_src/drivers/solo_driver:
5323!! The files here are include the _main driver that is used when
5324!! MOM is configured as an ocean-only model, as well as the files
5325!! that specify the surface forcing in this configuration.
5326!!
5327!! * config_src/external:
5328!! The files here are mostly just stubs, so that MOM6 can compile
5329!! with calls to the public interfaces external packages, but
5330!! without actually requiring those packages themselves. In more
5331!! elaborate configurations, would be linked to the actual code for
5332!! those external packages rather than these simple stubs.
5333!!
5334!! * config_src/memory/dynamic-symmetric:
5335!! The only file here is the version of MOM_memory.h that is used
5336!! for dynamic memory configurations of MOM.
5337!!
5338!! The directories under src contain most of the MOM files. These
5339!! files are used in every configuration using MOM.
5340!!
5341!! * src/core:
5342!! The files here constitute the MOM dynamic core. This directory
5343!! also includes files with the types that describe the model's
5344!! lateral grid and have defined types that are shared across
5345!! various MOM modules to allow for more succinct and flexible
5346!! subroutine argument lists.
5347!!
5348!! * src/diagnostics:
5349!! The files here calculate various diagnostics that are ancilliary
5350!! to the model itself. While most of these diagnostics do not
5351!! directly affect the model's solution, there are some, like the
5352!! calculation of the deformation radius, that are used in some
5353!! of the process parameterizations.
5354!!
5355!! * src/equation_of_state:
5356!! These files describe the physical properties of sea-water,
5357!! including both the equation of state and when it freezes.
5358!!
5359!! * src/framework:
5360!! These files provide infrastructure utilities for MOM. Many are
5361!! simply wrappers for capabilities provided by FMS, although others
5362!! provide capabilities (like the file_parser) that are unique to
5363!! MOM. When MOM is adapted to use a modeling infrastructure
5364!! distinct from FMS, most of the required changes are in this
5365!! directory.
5366!!
5367!! * src/initialization:
5368!! These are the files that are used to initialize the MOM grid
5369!! or provide the initial physical state for MOM. These files are
5370!! not intended to be modified, but provide a means for calling
5371!! user-specific initialization code like the examples in src/user.
5372!!
5373!! * src/parameterizations/lateral:
5374!! These files implement a number of quasi-lateral (along-layer)
5375!! process parameterizations, including lateral viscosities,
5376!! parameterizations of eddy effects, and the calculation of tidal
5377!! forcing.
5378!!
5379!! * src/parameterizations/vertical:
5380!! These files implement a number of vertical mixing or diabatic
5381!! processes, including the effects of vertical viscosity and
5382!! code to parameterize the planetary boundary layer. There is a
5383!! separate driver that orchestrates this portion of the algorithm,
5384!! and there is a diversity of parameterizations to be found here.
5385!!
5386!! * src/tracer:
5387!! These files handle the lateral transport and diffusion of
5388!! tracers, or are the code to implement various passive tracer
5389!! packages. Additional tracer packages are readily accommodated.
5390!!
5391!! * src/user:
5392!! These are either stub routines that a user could use to change
5393!! the model's initial conditions or forcing, or are examples that
5394!! implement specific test cases. These files can easily be hand
5395!! edited to create new analytically specified configurations.
5396!!
5397!!
5398!! Most simulations can be set up by modifying only the files
5399!! MOM_input, and possibly one or two of the files in src/user.
5400!! In addition, the diag_table (MOM_diag_table) will commonly be
5401!! modified to tailor the output to the needs of the question at
5402!! hand. The FMS utility mkmf works with a file called path_names
5403!! to build an appropriate makefile, and path_names should be edited
5404!! to reflect the actual location of the desired source code.
5405!!
5406!! The separate MOM-examples git repository provides a large number
5407!! of working configurations of MOM, along with reference solutions for several
5408!! different compilers on GFDL's latest large computer. The versions
5409!! of MOM_memory.h in these directories need not be used if dynamic
5410!! memory allocation is desired, and the answers should be unchanged.
5411!!
5412!!
5413!! There are 3 publicly visible subroutines in this file (MOM.F90).
5414!! * step_MOM steps MOM over a specified interval of time.
5415!! * MOM_initialize calls initialize and does other initialization
5416!! that does not warrant user modification.
5417!! * extract_surface_state determines the surface (bulk mixed layer
5418!! if traditional isopycnal vertical coordinate) properties of the
5419!! current model state and packages pointers to these fields into an
5420!! exported structure.
5421!!
5422!! The remaining subroutines in this file (src/core/MOM.F90) are:
5423!! * find_total_transport determines the barotropic mass transport.
5424!! * register_diags registers many diagnostic fields for the dynamic
5425!! solver, or of the main model variables.
5426!! * MOM_timing_init initializes various CPU time clocks.
5427!! * write_static_fields writes out various time-invariant fields.
5428!! * set_restart_fields is used to specify those fields that are
5429!! written to and read from the restart file.
5430!!
5431!! \section section_heat_budget Diagnosing MOM heat budget
5432!!
5433!! Here are some example heat budgets for the ALE version of MOM6.
5434!!
5435!! \subsection subsection_2d_heat_budget Depth integrated heat budget
5436!!
5437!! Depth integrated heat budget diagnostic for MOM.
5438!!
5439!! * OPOTTEMPTEND_2d = T_ADVECTION_XY_2d + OPOTTEMPPMDIFF_2d + HFDS + HFGEOU
5440!!
5441!! * T_ADVECTION_XY_2d = horizontal advection
5442!! * OPOTTEMPPMDIFF_2d = neutral diffusion
5443!! * HFDS = net surface boundary heat flux
5444!! * HFGEOU = geothermal heat flux
5445!!
5446!! * HFDS = net surface boundary heat flux entering the ocean
5447!! = rsntds + rlntds + hfls + hfss + heat_pme + hfsifrazil
5448!!
5449!! * More heat flux cross-checks
5450!! * hfds = net_heat_coupler + hfsifrazil + heat_pme
5451!! * heat_pme = heat_content_surfwater
5452!! = heat_content_massin + heat_content_massout
5453!! = heat_content_fprec + heat_content_cond + heat_content_vprec
5454!! + hfrunoffds + hfevapds + hfrainds
5455!!
5456!! \subsection subsection_3d_heat_budget Depth integrated heat budget
5457!!
5458!! Here is an example 3d heat budget diagnostic for MOM.
5459!!
5460!! * OPOTTEMPTEND = T_ADVECTION_XY + TH_TENDENCY_VERT_REMAP + OPOTTEMPDIFF + OPOTTEMPPMDIFF
5461!! + BOUNDARY_FORCING_HEAT_TENDENCY + FRAZIL_HEAT_TENDENCY
5462!!
5463!! * OPOTTEMPTEND = net tendency of heat as diagnosed in MOM.F90
5464!! * T_ADVECTION_XY = heating of a cell from lateral advection
5465!! * TH_TENDENCY_VERT_REMAP = heating of a cell from vertical remapping
5466!! * OPOTTEMPDIFF = heating of a cell from diabatic diffusion
5467!! * OPOTTEMPPMDIFF = heating of a cell from neutral diffusion
5468!! * BOUNDARY_FORCING_HEAT_TENDENCY = heating of cell from boundary fluxes
5469!! * FRAZIL_HEAT_TENDENCY = heating of cell from frazil
5470!!
5471!! * TH_TENDENCY_VERT_REMAP has zero vertical sum, as it redistributes heat in vertical.
5472!!
5473!! * OPOTTEMPDIFF has zero vertical sum, as it redistributes heat in the vertical.
5474!!
5475!! * BOUNDARY_FORCING_HEAT_TENDENCY generally has 3d structure, with k > 1 contributions from
5476!! penetrative shortwave, and from other fluxes for the case when layers are tiny, in which
5477!! case MOM6 partitions tendencies into k > 1 layers.
5478!!
5479!! * FRAZIL_HEAT_TENDENCY generally has 3d structure, since MOM6 frazil calculation checks the
5480!! full ocean column.
5481!!
5482!! * FRAZIL_HEAT_TENDENCY[k=\@sum] = HFSIFRAZIL = column integrated frazil heating.
5483!!
5484!! * HFDS = FRAZIL_HEAT_TENDENCY[k=\@sum] + BOUNDARY_FORCING_HEAT_TENDENCY[k=\@sum]
5485!!
5486!! Here is an example 2d heat budget (depth summed) diagnostic for MOM.
5487!!
5488!! * OPOTTEMPTEND_2d = T_ADVECTION_XY_2d + OPOTTEMPPMDIFF_2d + HFDS
5489!!
5490!!
5491!! Here is an example 3d salt budget diagnostic for MOM.
5492!!
5493!! * OSALTTEND = S_ADVECTION_XY + SH_TENDENCY_VERT_REMAP + OSALTDIFF + OSALTPMDIFF
5494!! + BOUNDARY_FORCING_SALT_TENDENCY
5495!!
5496!! * OSALTTEND = net tendency of salt as diagnosed in MOM.F90
5497!! * S_ADVECTION_XY = salt convergence to cell from lateral advection
5498!! * SH_TENDENCY_VERT_REMAP = salt convergence to cell from vertical remapping
5499!! * OSALTDIFF = salt convergence to cell from diabatic diffusion
5500!! * OSALTPMDIFF = salt convergence to cell from neutral diffusion
5501!! * BOUNDARY_FORCING_SALT_TENDENCY = salt convergence to cell from boundary fluxes
5502!!
5503!! * SH_TENDENCY_VERT_REMAP has zero vertical sum, as it redistributes salt in vertical.
5504!!
5505!! * OSALTDIFF has zero vertical sum, as it redistributes salt in the vertical.
5506!!
5507!! * BOUNDARY_FORCING_SALT_TENDENCY generally has 3d structure, with k > 1 contributions from
5508!! the case when layers are tiny, in which case MOM6 partitions tendencies into k > 1 layers.
5509!!
5510!! * SFDSI = BOUNDARY_FORCING_SALT_TENDENCY[k=\@sum]
5511!!
5512!! Here is an example 2d salt budget (depth summed) diagnostic for MOM.
5513!!
5514!! * OSALTTEND_2d = S_ADVECTION_XY_2d + OSALTPMDIFF_2d + SFDSI (+ SALT_FLUX_RESTORE)
5515!!
5516!!
5517!!
55180end module MOM
